Here the third hundred names are explained. yugāvartaḥ: as Time he turns the ages, Kṛta and the rest. naikamāyaḥ: he bears not one māyā but many (the n is not dropped by ‘nalopo nañaḥ’, Pāṇini 6.3.73, since there is also a negative na without the marker ñ). mahāśanaḥ: at the end of the kalpa he swallows all; great is his eating. adṛśyaḥ: beyond the reach of all the organs of knowledge. vyaktarūpaḥ: in his gross form his form is manifest; or, being self-luminous, it is manifest to yogins. sahasrajit: he conquers thousands of asuras in battle. anantajit: by his unthinkable power he conquers all beings everywhere, in battle and in play.

इष्टोऽविशिष्टः शिष्टेष्टः शिखण्डी नहुषो वृषः । क्रोधहा क्रोधकृत्कर्ता विश्वबाहुर्महीधरः ॥ ४७ ॥
iṣṭo 'viśiṣṭaḥ śiṣṭeṣṭaḥ śikhaṇḍī nahuṣo vṛṣaḥ | krodhahā krodhakṛt kartā viśvabāhur mahīdharaḥ || 47 ||
Padaccheda
इष्टः अविशिष्टः शिष्ट-इष्टः शिखण्डी नहुषः वृषः क्रोध-हा क्रोध-कृत् कर्ता विश्व-बाहुः मही-धरः
iṣṭaḥ aviśiṣṭaḥ śiṣṭa-iṣṭaḥ śikhaṇḍī nahuṣaḥ vṛṣaḥ krodha-hā krodha-kṛt kartā viśva-bāhuḥ mahī-dharaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Iṣṭa, beloved (as supreme bliss, or worshipped by sacrifice); Aviśiṣṭa, the same (as the inner controller of all); Śiṣṭeṣṭa, dear to the wise; Śikhaṇḍī, (as the cowherd) with the peacock feather; Nahuṣa, who binds beings by māyā; Vṛṣa, dharma; Krodhahā, who slays the anger of the good; Krodhakṛt, who rouses anger against the wicked; Kartā, the maker (of the world); Viśvabāhu, whose arms are everywhere; Mahīdhara, who holds the earth (or worship).

Bhāṣya Iṣṭaḥ: dear, being supreme bliss; or worshipped by sacrifice. aviśiṣṭaḥ: being the inner controller of all, the same in all. śiṣṭeṣṭaḥ: dear to the wise; or the wise are dear to him (‘I am exceedingly dear to the man of knowledge, and he to me’, Gītā 7.17); or worshipped by the wise. śikhaṇḍī: whose ornament is a peacock feather, in the dress of a cowherd. nahuṣaḥ: he binds (nah) beings by māyā. vṛṣaḥ: dharma, as raining desires (‘the Lord is known as dharma, vṛṣa, in the worlds’, Mahābhārata). krodhahā: he slays the anger of the good. krodhakṛt: he makes anger against the wicked. kartā: the maker of the world, the work (‘he who is the maker of these persons, whose work this is’, Kauṣītaki 4.19); or krodhakṛt-kartā, the cutter of those who make anger, the daityas, as one name. viśvabāhuḥ: as the support of all, or whose arms are everywhere (‘arms on every side’). mahīdharaḥ: he holds worship (mahī), or the earth.

अच्युतः प्रथितः प्राणः प्राणदो वासवानुजः । अपां निधिरधिष्ठानमप्रमत्तः प्रतिष्ठितः ॥ ४८ ॥
acyutaḥ prathitaḥ prāṇaḥ prāṇado vāsavānujaḥ | apāṃ nidhir adhiṣṭhānam apramattaḥ pratiṣṭhitaḥ || 48 ||
Padaccheda
अच्युतः प्रथितः प्राणः प्राण-दः वासव-अनुजः अपाम् निधिः अधिष्ठानम् अप्रमत्तः प्रतिष्ठितः
acyutaḥ prathitaḥ prāṇaḥ prāṇa-daḥ vāsava-anujaḥ apām nidhiḥ adhiṣṭhānam apramattaḥ pratiṣṭhitaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Acyuta, free of the six changes; Prathita, renowned (for creating and the rest); Prāṇa, (who as the thread-self moves creatures); Prāṇada, who gives (or takes) strength; Vāsavānuja, born after Indra (of Aditi); the Treasure-house of the waters (the ocean); Adhiṣṭhāna, the ground (of beings, as Brahman); Apramatta, never heedless (in giving fruits); Pratiṣṭhita, established in his own greatness.

Bhāṣya Acyutaḥ: free of the six changes of being (‘everlasting, auspicious, unfallen’). prathitaḥ: renowned for the work of creating the world and the rest. prāṇaḥ: as the thread-self (sūtrātman) he moves creatures (‘I am prāṇa’, the Ṛgvedins). prāṇadaḥ: he gives strength (prāṇa) to gods and asuras, or takes it away. vāsavānujaḥ: born of Aditi and Kaśyapa as the younger brother of Vāsava (Indra). apāṃ nidhiḥ: where the waters are stored (‘of lakes I am the ocean’, Gītā 10.24). adhiṣṭhānam: Brahman, on which beings rest as on their material cause (‘all beings rest in me’, Gītā 9.4). apramattaḥ: he is never careless in giving to those entitled the fruit their acts deserve. pratiṣṭhitaḥ: established in his own greatness (Chāndogya 7.24.1).

स्कन्दः स्कन्दधरो धुर्यो वरदो वायुवाहनः । वासुदेवो बृहद्भानुरादिदेवः पुरंदरः ॥ ४९ ॥
skandaḥ skandadharo dhuryo varado vāyuvāhanaḥ | vāsudevo bṛhadbhānur ādidevaḥ puraṃdaraḥ || 49 ||
Padaccheda
स्कन्दः स्कन्द-धरः धुर्यः वर-दः वायु-वाहनः वासुदेवः बृहत्-भानुः आदि-देवः पुरंदरः
skandaḥ skanda-dharaḥ dhuryaḥ vara-daḥ vāyu-vāhanaḥ vāsudevaḥ bṛhat-bhānuḥ ādi-devaḥ puraṃdaraḥ
Word by word
(He is) Skanda, who flows as nectar (or dries as wind); Skandadhara, who upholds the path of dharma; Dhurya, who bears the burden (of the birth and the rest of all beings); Varada, giver of boons; Vāyuvāhana, who drives the seven winds; Vāsudeva, (who covers all and in whom all dwell, the shining one); Bṛhadbhānu, whose great rays (in sun and moon) light the world; Ādideva, the first cause and god; Puraṃdara, who destroyed the cities of the gods' foes.

Bhāṣya Skandaḥ: he flows as nectar, or dries up as wind. skandadharaḥ: he upholds skanda, the path of dharma. dhuryaḥ: he bears the yoke of the birth and the rest of all beings. varadaḥ: he gives the boons desired; or as the sacrificer gives the fee (vara) (‘the cow is the vara’). vāyuvāhanaḥ: he makes the seven winds, āvaha and the rest, blow.

Bhāṣya Vāsudevaḥ: vāsu, he dwells (vasati) or makes dwell, or covers all; deva, he shines, plays, wishes to conquer, deals, goes; both together (‘I cover the whole world like the sun with its rays; I am the dwelling of all beings: so I am called Vāsudeva’; ‘from dwelling in all beings, from being Vasu, from the source of the gods, he is to be known as Vāsudeva’, Udyogaparvan; ‘because he dwells everywhere and all dwells in him, the wise call him Vāsudeva’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa). bṛhadbhānuḥ: whose great rays, in the moon, the sun and the rest, light the world. ādidevaḥ: the first, the cause, and god, having shining and the other qualities. puraṃdaraḥ: he broke the cities of the foes of the gods (Pāṇini 6.3.69).

अशोकस्तारणस्तारः शूरः शौरिर्जनेश्वरः । अनुकूलः शतावर्तः पद्मी पद्मनिभेक्षणः ॥ ५० ॥
aśokas tāraṇas tāraḥ śūraḥ śaurir janeśvaraḥ | anukūlaḥ śatāvartaḥ padmī padmanibhekṣaṇaḥ || 50 ||
Padaccheda
अशोकः तारणः तारः शूरः शौरिः जन-ईश्वरः अनुकूलः शत-आवर्तः पद्मी पद्म-निभ-ईक्षणः
aśokaḥ tāraṇaḥ tāraḥ śūraḥ śauriḥ jana-īśvaraḥ anukūlaḥ śata-āvartaḥ padmī padma-nibha-īkṣaṇaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Aśoka, untouched by sorrow and the other five waves; Tāraṇa, who carries (beings) across the ocean of the round; Tāra, who saves (from the fear of womb, birth, age and death); Śūra, the Valiant; Śauri, son of Vasudeva of Śūra's line; Janeśvara, the Lord of creatures; Anukūla, well-disposed (to all, being their Self); Śatāvarta, of a hundred appearances; Padmī, lotus in hand; Padmanibhekṣaṇa, whose eyes are like lotuses.

Bhāṣya Aśokaḥ: free of the six waves, sorrow and the rest. tāraṇaḥ: he carries across the ocean of the round. tāraḥ: he carries across from fear, of the womb, birth, old age and death. śūraḥ: from valour. śauriḥ: the son of Vasudeva, a descendant of Śūra. janeśvaraḥ: lord of creatures. anukūlaḥ: being the Self of all, he is well disposed to all; none acts against himself. śatāvartaḥ: for the protection of dharma he has a hundred appearances; or he dwells in the hundred channels as prāṇa. padmī: a lotus is in his hand. padmanibhekṣaṇaḥ: his eyes are like lotuses.

पद्मनाभोऽरविन्दाक्षः पद्मगर्भः शरीरभृत् । महर्द्धिरृद्धो वृद्धात्मा महाक्षो गरुडध्वजः ॥ ५१ ॥
padmanābho 'ravindākṣaḥ padmagarbhaḥ śarīrabhṛt | maharddhir ṛddho vṛddhātmā mahākṣo garuḍadhvajaḥ || 51 ||
Padaccheda
पद्म-नाभः अरविन्द-अक्षः पद्म-गर्भः शरीर-भृत् महा-ऋद्धिः ऋद्धः वृद्ध-आत्मा महा-अक्षः गरुड-ध्वजः
padma-nābhaḥ aravinda-akṣaḥ padma-garbhaḥ śarīra-bhṛt mahā-ṛddhiḥ ṛddhaḥ vṛddha-ātmā mahā-akṣaḥ garuḍa-dhvajaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Padmanābha, (abiding in the centre of the lotus); Aravindākṣa, lotus-eyed; Padmagarbha, (to be worshipped) within the lotus of the heart; Śarīrabhṛt, who sustains bodies (as food and breath); Maharddhi, of great glory; Ṛddha, grown great (as the manifold); Vṛddhātmā, whose Self is ancient; Mahākṣa, of great eyes; Garuḍadhvaja, whose banner bears Garuḍa.

Bhāṣya Padmanābhaḥ: he abides in the navel, the pericarp, of the lotus. aravindākṣaḥ: whose eyes are like lotuses. padmagarbhaḥ: to be worshipped within the lotus called the heart. śarīrabhṛt: nourishing as food or breath, he sustains the bodies of the embodied; or by his own māyā he bears bodies. maharddhiḥ: his glory is great. ṛddhaḥ: he has grown, in the form of the manifold. vṛddhātmā: whose self is old, ancient. mahākṣaḥ: two great eyes, or many great eyes, are his. garuḍadhvajaḥ: his banner is marked with Garuḍa.

अतुलः शरभो भीमः समयज्ञो हविर्हरिः । सर्वलक्षणलक्षण्यो लक्ष्मीवान्समितिंजयः ॥ ५२ ॥
atulaḥ śarabho bhīmaḥ samayajño havirhariḥ | sarvalakṣaṇalakṣaṇyo lakṣmīvān samitiṃjayaḥ || 52 ||
Padaccheda
अतुलः शरभः भीमः समय-ज्ञः हविः-हरिः सर्व-लक्षण-लक्षण्यः लक्ष्मीवान् समितिंजयः
atulaḥ śarabhaḥ bhīmaḥ samaya-jñaḥ haviḥ-hariḥ sarva-lakṣaṇa-lakṣaṇyaḥ lakṣmīvān samitiṃjayaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Atula, without compare; Śarabha, (shining as the inner Self in the bodies that perish); Bhīma, whom all fear; Samayajña, who knows the times (of creation, preservation and dissolution); Havirhari, who takes the share of the oblation; Sarvalakṣaṇalakṣaṇya, the one known by all the means of knowledge; Lakṣmīvān, on whose breast Lakṣmī dwells; Samitiṃjaya, victor in battle.

Bhāṣya Atulaḥ: there is no measure (tulā) to compare him with (‘there is no likeness of him whose name is great glory’, Śvetāśvatara 4.19; ‘none is equal to you’, Gītā 11.43). śarabhaḥ: śara are bodies, since they perish; he shines in them as the inner Self. bhīmaḥ: all fear him (Pāṇini 3.4.74); or, to those on the right path, not terrible. samayajñaḥ: he knows the times of creation, preservation and dissolution; or the six seasons; or his worship is evenness in all beings (‘evenness is the worship of Acyuta’, Prahlāda).

Bhāṣya Havirhariḥ: he takes (harati) the share of the oblation in sacrifices (‘I am the enjoyer and lord of all sacrifices’, Gītā 9.24); or two names: haviḥ, he who is offered with oblation (‘they bound Puruṣa, the victim’), and hariḥ, who takes away the sin of those who remember him, or the round, or who is tawny (‘I take away the sin of those who remember me and the share of oblations; and my colour is tawny: so I am called Hari’). sarvalakṣaṇalakṣaṇyaḥ: the knowledge that arises from all the means of knowledge is lakṣaṇa; he is the one fit to be so known, being alone the highest truth. lakṣmīvān: Lakṣmī dwells always on his breast. samitiṃjayaḥ: he wins in battle.

विक्षरो रोहितो मार्गो हेतुर्दामोदरः सहः । महीधरो महाभागो वेगवानमिताशनः ॥ ५३ ॥
vikṣaro rohito mārgo hetur dāmodaraḥ sahaḥ | mahīdharo mahābhāgo vegavān amitāśanaḥ || 53 ||
Padaccheda
विक्षरः रोहितः मार्गः हेतुः दामोदरः सहः मही-धरः महा-भागः वेगवान् अमित-अशनः
vikṣaraḥ rohitaḥ mārgaḥ hetuḥ dāmodaraḥ sahaḥ mahī-dharaḥ mahā-bhāgaḥ vegavān amita-aśanaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Vikṣara, without decay; Rohita, (taking by his will) the red form of the Fish; Mārga, the Way (sought by seekers of liberation); Hetu, the cause, material and efficient; Dāmodara, (known by a mind made lofty by restraint, or bound with a rope at the belly); Saha, who overcomes and endures; Mahīdhara, (as the mountains) bearing the earth; Mahābhāga, of great enjoyments and fortune; Vegavān, swift; Amitāśana, who at dissolution eats the universe.

Bhāṣya Vikṣaraḥ: his perishing (kṣara) has gone. rohitaḥ: of his own will he takes a red form, the form of the fish. mārgaḥ: seekers of liberation seek (mārgayanti) him; or he is the way by which the supreme bliss is reached. hetuḥ: he alone is the cause, material and efficient. dāmodaraḥ: his udara, his mind, made lofty by dama and the other means, is the means by which he is reached (‘they know Dāmodara from dama’, Mahābhārata); or he was bound at the belly (udara) with a rope (dāman) by Yaśodā (Brahma Purāṇa); or ‘the dāmas are the names of the worlds; they are within his belly’ (Vyāsa). sahaḥ: he overcomes all, or endures.

Bhāṣya Mahīdharaḥ: he holds the earth in the form of the mountains (‘the forests, the mountains and the quarters are Viṣṇu’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa). mahābhāgaḥ: taking a body by his own will, he enjoys great enjoyments; or his fortune in his appearances is great. vegavān: having speed (‘unmoving, one, swifter than the mind’, Īśa 4). amitāśanaḥ: at dissolution he eats the universe.

उद्भवः क्षोभणो देवः श्रीगर्भः परमेश्वरः । करणं कारणं कर्ता विकर्ता गहनो गुहः ॥ ५४ ॥
udbhavaḥ kṣobhaṇo devaḥ śrīgarbhaḥ parameśvaraḥ | karaṇaṃ kāraṇaṃ kartā vikartā gahano guhaḥ || 54 ||
Padaccheda
उद्भवः क्षोभणः देवः श्री-गर्भः परम-ईश्वरः करणम् कारणम् कर्ता विकर्ता गहनः गुहः
udbhavaḥ kṣobhaṇaḥ devaḥ śrī-garbhaḥ parama-īśvaraḥ karaṇam kāraṇam kartā vikartā gahanaḥ guhaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Udbhava, the material cause of the manifold (or gone beyond the round); Kṣobhaṇa, who stirred Prakṛti and Puruṣa at creation; Deva, the God; Śrīgarbha, within whom the glory of the world lies; Parameśvara, the supreme Lord; Karaṇa, the instrument (of creating the world); Kāraṇa, its cause, material and efficient; Kartā, the free agent; Vikartā, maker of the varied world; Gahana, whose nature cannot be fathomed; Guha, who hides his nature by his māyā.

Bhāṣya Udbhavaḥ: as the material cause of the coming forth of the manifold; or he has gone up beyond the round. kṣobhaṇaḥ: at creation he entered Prakṛti and Puruṣa and stirred them (Viṣṇu Purāṇa). devaḥ: he shines, plays in creation and the rest, wishes to conquer the asuras, deals in all beings, shines as the Self, is praised, goes everywhere (‘one god’, Śvetāśvatara 6.11). śrīgarbhaḥ: his glory, the world, lies within him. parameśvaraḥ: supreme and ruling (‘the supreme Lord, abiding the same in all beings’, Gītā 13.27). karaṇam: the most effective means in creating the world. kāraṇam: the cause, material and efficient. kartā: independent. vikartā: he makes the varied world. gahanaḥ: his nature, power and acts cannot be known. guhaḥ: he hides his nature by his māyā.

व्यवसायो व्यवस्थानः संस्थानः स्थानदो ध्रुवः । परर्द्धिः परमस्पष्टस्तुष्टः पुष्टः शुभेक्षणः ॥ ५५ ॥
vyavasāyo vyavasthānaḥ saṃsthānaḥ sthānado dhruvaḥ | pararddhiḥ paramaspaṣṭas tuṣṭaḥ puṣṭaḥ śubhekṣaṇaḥ || 55 ||
Padaccheda
व्यवसायः व्यवस्थानः संस्थानः स्थान-दः ध्रुवः पर-ऋद्धिः परम-स्पष्टः तुष्टः पुष्टः शुभ-ईक्षणः
vyavasāyaḥ vyavasthānaḥ saṃsthānaḥ sthāna-daḥ dhruvaḥ para-ṛddhiḥ parama-spaṣṭaḥ tuṣṭaḥ puṣṭaḥ śubha-īkṣaṇaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Vyavasāya, pure knowing; Vyavasthāna, in whom all is ordered (and who orders the stations and classes); Saṃsthāna, in whom beings come to rest at dissolution; Sthānada, who gives (Dhruva and others) their stations; Dhruva, imperishable; Pararddhi, of supreme glory; Paramaspaṣṭa, supreme, and evident as consciousness; Tuṣṭa, content (being bliss); Puṣṭa, full; Śubhekṣaṇa, whose glance is auspicious.

Bhāṣya Vyavasāyaḥ: being pure knowing. vyavasthānaḥ: all is set in order in him; or he arranges the offices of the guardians of the worlds, the kinds of creatures born from womb, egg and sweat, the classes brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya, vaiśya, śūdra and the mixed, and the stages of life with their duties. saṃsthānaḥ: the coming to rest of beings, dissolution, is in him; or his station is excellent. sthānadaḥ: he gives Dhruva and the others places fitting their deeds. dhruvaḥ: being imperishable. pararddhiḥ: his glory is supreme. paramaspaṣṭaḥ: parama, whose beauty (mā) is supreme, or highest of all, his being depending on nothing else; and spaṣṭa, evident as consciousness. tuṣṭaḥ: being one with supreme bliss. puṣṭaḥ: being full in himself. śubhekṣaṇaḥ: his glance is auspicious: it gives liberation to seekers of liberation, enjoyments to those who seek them, cuts all doubts, purifies sinners, cuts the knot of the heart, exhausts all action and ends ignorance (‘the knot of the heart is cut’, Muṇḍaka 2.2.9).

रामो विरामो विरतो मार्गो नेयो नयोऽनयः । वीरः शक्तिमतां श्रेष्ठो धर्मो धर्मविदुत्तमः ॥ ५६ ॥
rāmo virāmo virato mārgo neyo nayo 'nayaḥ | vīraḥ śaktimatāṃ śreṣṭho dharmo dharmavid uttamaḥ || 56 ||
Padaccheda
रामः विरामः विरतः मार्गः नेयः नयः अनयः वीरः शक्तिमताम् श्रेष्ठः धर्मः धर्म-वित् उत्तमः
rāmaḥ virāmaḥ virataḥ mārgaḥ neyaḥ nayaḥ anayaḥ vīraḥ śaktimatām śreṣṭhaḥ dharmaḥ dharma-vit uttamaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Rāma, in whom yogins delight (and the son of Daśaratha); Virāma, in whom beings come to rest; Virata, without attachment to objects; Mārga, the Way (to immortality); Neya, (the Self) to whom the jīva is led; Naya, the Leader; Anaya, who has no leader; Vīra, the Valiant; the best of the mighty; Dharma, (who upholds all beings); the highest of the knowers of dharma.

Bhāṣya Rāmaḥ: yogins delight (ramante) in him, whose nature is eternal bliss (‘the supreme Brahman, the Self of consciousness and eternal bliss in which yogins delight, is named by the word rāma’, Padma Purāṇa); or Rāma, son of Daśaratha, who by his own will took a lovely form. virāmaḥ: in him beings come to their end. virataḥ: his attachment to the enjoyment of objects has gone. mārgaḥ: knowing him, seekers of liberation become fit for immortality; he alone is the path (‘there is no other path for going’, Śvetāśvatara 3.8). neyaḥ: by the way, right knowledge, the jīva is led to be the supreme Self. nayaḥ: who leads, the leader; way, led-to and leader are three forms of him. anayaḥ: he has no leader. Here the fourth hundred names are explained.

Bhāṣya Vīraḥ: from valour. śaktimatāṃ śreṣṭhaḥ: mightier even than the mighty, Viriñci and the rest. dharmaḥ: as upholding (dhāraṇa) all beings (‘this subtle dharma’, Kaṭha 1.1.21); or he is worshipped by dharmas. dharmavid uttamaḥ: śruti and smṛti are his commands, so he is the highest of all the knowers of dharma.

Reading Many editions read virajaḥ here; the edition reads virataḥ, and Śaṅkara explains virataḥ.

वैकुण्ठः पुरुषः प्राणः प्राणदः प्रणवः पृथुः । हिरण्यगर्भः शत्रुघ्नो व्याप्तो वायुरधोक्षजः ॥ ५७ ॥
vaikuṇṭhaḥ puruṣaḥ prāṇaḥ prāṇadaḥ praṇavaḥ pṛthuḥ | hiraṇyagarbhaḥ śatrughno vyāpto vāyur adhokṣajaḥ || 57 ||
Padaccheda
वैकुण्ठः पुरुषः प्राणः प्राण-दः प्रणवः पृथुः हिरण्य-गर्भः शत्रु-घ्नः व्याप्तः वायुः अधोक्षजः
vaikuṇṭhaḥ puruṣaḥ prāṇaḥ prāṇa-daḥ praṇavaḥ pṛthuḥ hiraṇya-garbhaḥ śatru-ghnaḥ vyāptaḥ vāyuḥ adhokṣajaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Vaikuṇṭha, (who at the beginning held the elements together, checking their course); Puruṣa, (prior to all, burning all sins, lying in the city of the body); Prāṇa, (who moves as the knower of the field and as breath); Prāṇada, who cuts off the breath (at dissolution); Praṇava, Om; Pṛthu, vast (as the manifold); Hiraṇyagarbha, (whose womb is the golden egg); Śatrughna, slayer of the foes of the gods; Vyāpta, pervading (as cause); Vāyu, (who makes smell); Adhokṣaja, who never wanes (or is known when the senses are turned within).

Bhāṣya Vaikuṇṭhaḥ: vikuṇṭhā is the checking of movement; at the beginning of the world he joined the elements together and checked their course (‘I joined earth with water, space with wind, and wind with light: so I am Vaikuṇṭha’, Śāntiparvan). puruṣaḥ: from abiding (sadana) before (purā) all, or from destroying (sādana) all sin (‘because he was before all this and burnt all sins’, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 1.4.1); or from lying in the city (‘this Puruṣa lies in all cities’, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 2.5.18). prāṇaḥ: he breathes, as the knower of the field, or moves beings as breath (Viṣṇu Purāṇa). prāṇadaḥ: he cuts off the breath of beings at dissolution. praṇavaḥ: he sounds forth (praṇauti) (‘therefore one sounds Om’); or he is bowed to (‘the Vedas bow to him here, so he is called praṇava’, Sanatkumāra). pṛthuḥ: spread out as the manifold.

Bhāṣya Hiraṇyagarbhaḥ: the golden egg, the cause of Hiraṇyagarbha's birth, sprung from his seed, is his womb. śatrughnaḥ: he slays the enemies of the gods. vyāptaḥ: as the cause he pervades all effects. vāyuḥ: he makes smell (‘I am the pure smell in the earth’, Gītā 7.9). adhokṣajaḥ: ‘because he never wanes downward’ (Udyogaparvan); or because he was born between heaven (akṣa) and earth (adhaḥ) as Virāj; or ‘when the host of the senses is turned down and drawn inward, knowledge of him arises: so he is called Adhokṣaja’.

ऋतुः सुदर्शनः कालः परमेष्ठी परिग्रहः । उग्रः संवत्सरो दक्षो विश्रामो विश्वदक्षिणः ॥ ५८ ॥
ṛtuḥ sudarśanaḥ kālaḥ parameṣṭhī parigrahaḥ | ugraḥ saṃvatsaro dakṣo viśrāmo viśvadakṣiṇaḥ || 58 ||
Padaccheda
ऋतुः सु-दर्शनः कालः परमेष्ठी परिग्रहः उग्रः संवत्सरः दक्षः विश्रामः विश्व-दक्षिणः
ṛtuḥ su-darśanaḥ kālaḥ parameṣṭhī parigrahaḥ ugraḥ saṃvatsaraḥ dakṣaḥ viśrāmaḥ viśva-dakṣiṇaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Ṛtu, (Time, marked by the seasons); Sudarśana, whose knowledge gives the fruit of liberation (whose eyes are lotus-long, easily seen by devotees); Kāla, Time; Parameṣṭhī, abiding in his own greatness in the space of the heart; Parigraha, (grasped on every side by those who seek refuge); Ugra, (fearsome even to the sun); Saṃvatsara, in whom beings dwell; Dakṣa, able (growing as the world); Viśrāma, who gives rest (liberation); Viśvadakṣiṇa, the most skilful in all.

Bhāṣya Ṛtuḥ: as Time he is marked by the word ‘season’. sudarśanaḥ: his knowledge (darśana), whose fruit is liberation, is auspicious; or his eyes are long like lotus petals; or he is easily seen by devotees. kālaḥ: he reckons all (‘of reckoners I am Time’, Gītā 10.30). parameṣṭhī: he is wont to abide in his own supreme greatness, in the space of the heart (‘Parameṣṭhin shines’). parigrahaḥ: being everywhere, he is grasped on every side by those who seek refuge; or known on every side; or he accepts the leaf, flower and the like offered by devotees. ugraḥ: he is the cause of fear even to the sun and the rest (‘from fear of him the sun rises’, Taittirīya 2.8). saṃvatsaraḥ: beings dwell (saṃvasanti) in him. dakṣaḥ: growing in the form of the world; or doing all acts quickly. viśrāmaḥ: to those who long for rest in the ocean of the round, tossed by the six waves, hunger, thirst and the rest, and tormented by the great afflictions, ignorance and the rest, and the lesser ones, pride and the rest, he gives rest, liberation. viśvadakṣiṇaḥ: abler than all, or skilful in all acts.

विस्तारः स्थावरस्थाणुः प्रमाणं बीजमव्ययम् । अर्थोऽनर्थो महाकोशो महाभोगो महाधनः ॥ ५९ ॥
vistāraḥ sthāvarasthāṇuḥ pramāṇaṃ bījam avyayam | artho 'nartho mahākośo mahābhogo mahādhanaḥ || 59 ||
Padaccheda
विस्तारः स्थावर-स्थाणुः प्रमाणम् बीजम् अव्ययम् अर्थः अनर्थः महा-कोशः महा-भोगः महा-धनः
vistāraḥ sthāvara-sthāṇuḥ pramāṇam bījam avyayam arthaḥ anarthaḥ mahā-kośaḥ mahā-bhogaḥ mahā-dhanaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Vistāra, in whom all the worlds are spread out; Sthāvarasthāṇu, abiding, and the ground in which earth and the rest abide; Pramāṇa, (consciousness, the means of knowing); the undecaying Seed; Artha, the end sought by all (being happiness); Anartha, who has no end to seek; Mahākośa, (covered by the great sheaths, food and the rest); Mahābhoga, whose enjoyment (bliss) is great; Mahādhana, whose wealth (the means of enjoyment) is great.

Bhāṣya Vistāraḥ: all the worlds are spread out in him. sthāvarasthāṇuḥ: sthāvara, abiding by nature; sthāṇu, in whom earth and the other abiding things abide; one name. pramāṇam: as consciousness. bījam avyayam: the cause that does not become otherwise; one name with its qualifier. arthaḥ: being happiness, he is sought (arthyate) by all. anarthaḥ: having all his desires, he has nothing to gain. mahākośaḥ: the great sheaths, food and the rest, cover him. mahābhogaḥ: his enjoyment, which is happiness, is great. mahādhanaḥ: his wealth, the means of enjoyment, is great.

अनिर्विण्णः स्थविष्ठोऽभूर्धर्मयूपो महामखः । नक्षत्रनेमिर्नक्षत्री क्षमः क्षामः समीहनः ॥ ६० ॥
anirviṇṇaḥ sthaviṣṭho 'bhūr dharmayūpo mahāmakhaḥ | nakṣatranemir nakṣatrī kṣamaḥ kṣāmaḥ samīhanaḥ || 60 ||
Padaccheda
अनिर्विण्णः स्थविष्ठः अभूः धर्म-यूपः महा-मखः नक्षत्र-नेमिः नक्षत्री क्षमः क्षामः समीहनः
anirviṇṇaḥ sthaviṣṭhaḥ abhūḥ dharma-yūpaḥ mahā-makhaḥ nakṣatra-nemiḥ nakṣatrī kṣamaḥ kṣāmaḥ samīhanaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Anirviṇṇa, never wearied (having all he desires); Sthaviṣṭha, the vastest (as Virāj); Abhū, unborn; Dharmayūpa, the post to which the dharmas that worship him are bound; Mahāmakha, (in whom sacrifices become great, giving liberation); Nakṣatranemi, (who turns the wheel of the stars); Nakṣatrī, (as the moon); Kṣama, able in all things (and patient); Kṣāma, (who remains when all changes have waned); Samīhana, who strives well (for creation and the rest).

Bhāṣya Anirviṇṇaḥ: having all his desires, he knows no weariness. sthaviṣṭhaḥ: standing as Virāj (‘fire is his head, the moon and sun his eyes’, Muṇḍaka 2.1.4). abhūḥ: unborn; or bhūḥ, being (from bhū with kvip), or the earth. dharmayūpaḥ: as the victim to the post, the dharmas that are his worship are bound to him. mahāmakhaḥ: the sacrifices offered to him become great, giving the fruit of liberation. nakṣatranemiḥ: Dhruva, at the tail of the porpoise of stars, turns the wheel of the lights; in the heart of that porpoise Viṣṇu stands like the nave of the wheel (‘Viṣṇu is the heart’, of the porpoise, Svādhyāya-brāhmaṇa). nakṣatrī: as the moon (‘among the stars I am the moon’, Gītā 10.21). kṣamaḥ: able in all acts; or forbearing (‘like the earth in patience’, Vālmīki). kṣāmaḥ: when all changes have waned, he remains in his own nature. samīhanaḥ: he strives rightly for creation and the rest.

Note The text has sthaviṣṭho 'bhūḥ; Śaṅkara takes it first as abhūḥ, ‘unborn’, then as bhūḥ.

यज्ञ इज्यो महेज्यश्च क्रतुः सत्रं सतां गतिः । सर्वदर्शी विमुक्तात्मा सर्वज्ञो ज्ञानमुत्तमम् ॥ ६१ ॥
yajña ijyo mahejyaś ca kratuḥ satraṃ satāṃ gatiḥ | sarvadarśī vimuktātmā sarvajño jñānam uttamam || 61 ||
Padaccheda
यज्ञः इज्यः महा-इज्यः च क्रतुः सत्रम् सताम् गतिः सर्व-दर्शी विमुक्त-आत्मा सर्व-ज्ञः ज्ञानम् उत्तमम्
yajñaḥ ijyaḥ mahā-ijyaḥ ca kratuḥ satram satām gatiḥ sarva-darśī vimukta-ātmā sarva-jñaḥ jñānam uttamam
Word by word
(He is) Yajña, the Sacrifice; Ijya, to be worshipped; and Mahejya, most to be worshipped (giving liberation); Kratu, the rite (with the sacrificial post); Satra, the session (or who protects the good); the Refuge of the good; Sarvadarśī, who sees all; Vimuktātmā, free by nature; Sarvajña, the All and the knower; the highest Knowledge.

Bhāṣya Yajñaḥ: being in the form of all sacrifices; or he goes forth as sacrifice to please all the gods (‘Viṣṇu is the sacrifice’). ijyaḥ: he alone is to be worshipped (‘those who worship gods and fathers with holy sacrifices worship Viṣṇu, the Self, by the self’, Harivaṃśa). mahejyaḥ: of all deities to be worshipped, he most, as the giver of liberation. kratuḥ: the sacrifice with a post. satram: the rite enjoined as a session; or he protects (trā) the good (sat). satāṃ gatiḥ: for the good who seek liberation there is no other goal. sarvadarśī: by his natural knowing he sees all the deeds of beings. vimuktātmā: whose self is free by nature, or the free Self (‘freed, he is freed’, Kaṭha 2.2.1). sarvajñaḥ: the All and the knower (‘all this is this Self’, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 2.4.6). jñānam uttamam: one name: the highest knowledge, unborn, unbounded, the best means of all, Brahman (‘Brahman is truth, knowledge, infinite’, Taittirīya 2.1).

सुव्रतः सुमुखः सूक्ष्मः सुघोषः सुखदः सुहृत् । मनोहरो जितक्रोधो वीरबाहुर्विदारणः ॥ ६२ ॥
suvrataḥ sumukhaḥ sūkṣmaḥ sughoṣaḥ sukhadaḥ suhṛt | manoharo jitakrodho vīrabāhur vidāraṇaḥ || 62 ||
Padaccheda
सु-व्रतः सु-मुखः सूक्ष्मः सु-घोषः सुख-दः सु-हृत् मनः-हरः जित-क्रोधः वीर-बाहुः विदारणः
su-vrataḥ su-mukhaḥ sūkṣmaḥ su-ghoṣaḥ sukha-daḥ su-hṛt manaḥ-haraḥ jita-krodhaḥ vīra-bāhuḥ vidāraṇaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Suvrata, of good vow (to give safety to all who come to him); Sumukha, of fair face; Sūkṣma, subtle; Sughoṣa, whose sound (the Veda) is fair; Sukhada, who gives happiness (to the good, and cuts it off for the wicked); Suhṛt, the friend (who helps seeking no return); Manohara, who captivates the mind (being bliss); Jitakrodha, who has conquered anger; Vīrabāhu, whose arms are valiant; Vidāraṇa, who rends the unrighteous.

Bhāṣya Suvrataḥ: whose vow is good (‘to one who comes to me once and asks, saying “I am yours”, I give safety from all: that is my vow’, Rāma in the Rāmāyaṇa). sumukhaḥ: whose face is fair (‘with a gracious face, eyes long like lotus petals’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa); or Rāma, son of Daśaratha, of glad face when sent to the forest (the Rāmāyaṇa verses: he accepted his father's word as gladly as the consecration); or by teaching all knowledge (Śvetāśvatara 6.18). sūkṣmaḥ: being free of sound and the rest, which make space and the other elements each grosser (‘all-pervading, most subtle’, Muṇḍaka 1.1.6). sughoṣaḥ: his sound, the Veda, is fair; or deep as thunder.

Bhāṣya Sukhadaḥ: he gives happiness to the well-behaved, or cuts it off for the ill-behaved. suhṛt: he helps without looking for a return. manoharaḥ: being unsurpassed bliss, he steals the mind (‘the infinite is happiness; there is no happiness in the small’, Chāndogya 7.23.1). jitakrodhaḥ: he has conquered anger; he slays the foes of the gods to keep the bounds of the Veda, not under the sway of anger. vīrabāhuḥ: keeping the bounds of the Veda by slaying the foes of the thirty gods, his arms are valiant. vidāraṇaḥ: he rends the unrighteous.

स्वापनः स्ववशो व्यापी नैकात्मा नैककर्मकृत् । वत्सरो वत्सलो वत्सी रत्नगर्भो धनेश्वरः ॥ ६३ ॥
svāpanaḥ svavaśo vyāpī naikātmā naikakarmakṛt | vatsaro vatsalo vatsī ratnagarbho dhaneśvaraḥ || 63 ||
Padaccheda
स्वापनः स्व-वशः व्यापी नैक-आत्मा नैक-कर्म-कृत् वत्सरः वत्सलः वत्सी रत्न-गर्भः धन-ईश्वरः
svāpanaḥ sva-vaśaḥ vyāpī naika-ātmā naika-karma-kṛt vatsaraḥ vatsalaḥ vatsī ratna-garbhaḥ dhana-īśvaraḥ
Word by word
(He is) Svāpana, who by māyā lulls beings (to forgetting their Self); Svavaśa, independent (the cause of the world); Vyāpī, pervading like space; Naikātmā, standing in many forms (as his glories); Naikakarmakṛt, who does the many works (of creation and the rest); Vatsara, in whom all dwells; Vatsala, loving to his devotees; Vatsī, who protects his calves (his creatures); Ratnagarbha, (the ocean) with jewels within; Dhaneśvara, the Lord of wealth.

Bhāṣya Svāpanaḥ: by māyā he makes beings void of the knowledge of their Self. svavaśaḥ: independent, being the cause of the world's rise, stay and end. vyāpī: pervading all like space (‘like space, all-pervading and eternal’); or as the cause pervading all effects. naikātmā: he stands in many ways as his glories, the powers that appear as the causes of creation and the rest. naikakarmakṛt: he does the works of the world's rise, wealth and ruin. vatsaraḥ: all dwells (vas) in him. vatsalaḥ: loving to his devotees (Pāṇini 5.2.98). vatsī: as he protects the calves; or creatures are calves to him, the father of the world. ratnagarbhaḥ: the ocean, with jewels in its womb. dhaneśvaraḥ: lord of riches.

धर्मगुब्धर्मकृद्धर्मी सदसत्क्षरमक्षरम् । अविज्ञाता सहस्रांशुर्विधाता कृतलक्षणः ॥ ६४ ॥
dharmagub dharmakṛd dharmī sad asat kṣaram akṣaram | avijñātā sahasrāṃśur vidhātā kṛtalakṣaṇaḥ || 64 ||
Padaccheda
धर्म-गुप् धर्म-कृत् धर्मी सत् असत् क्षरम् अक्षरम् अविज्ञाता सहस्र-अंशुः विधाता कृत-लक्षणः
dharma-gup dharma-kṛt dharmī sat asat kṣaram akṣaram avijñātā sahasra-aṃśuḥ vidhātā kṛta-lakṣaṇaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Dharmagup, who guards dharma; Dharmakṛt, who does dharma (to keep its bounds); Dharmī, who upholds the dharmas; Sat, the real (the supreme Brahman); Asat, (the lower Brahman, the modification); Kṣara, the perishable (all beings); Akṣara, the imperishable (the unchanging); Avijñātā, not the knower (bound by notions of agency, as the jīva is); Sahasrāṃśu, the thousand-rayed; Vidhātā, the supporter (of Śeṣa, the elephants of the quarters and the mountains); Kṛtalakṣaṇa, (whose nature is consciousness, maker of the śāstras and of the marks of things).

Bhāṣya Dharmagup: he guards dharma (Gītā 4.8). dharmakṛt: though beyond dharma and adharma, he does dharma to keep its bounds. dharmī: he upholds the dharmas. sat: the supreme Brahman, not false (‘being alone, dear one, was this in the beginning’, Chāndogya 6.2.1). asat: the lower Brahman (‘a modification is a name resting on speech’, Chāndogya 6.1.4). kṣaram: all beings; akṣaram: the unchanging (‘two puruṣas are in the world, the perishable and the imperishable; all beings are the perishable, the unchanging is called the imperishable’, Gītā 15.16).

Bhāṣya Avijñātā: the knowing that imagines agency and the like in the Self is a construction; the jīva, wrapped in its impressions, is the knower (vijñātā); Viṣṇu, unlike it, is not. sahasrāṃśuḥ: the rays in the sun and the rest are his, so he is the true thousand-rayed (‘by whose light the sun burns, kindled’; ‘the light in the sun’, Gītā 15.12). vidhātā: he supports in a special way Śeṣa, the elephants of the quarters and the mountains, which support all beings. kṛtalakṣaṇaḥ: his nature is ever-accomplished consciousness; or he made the marks, the śāstras (‘the Vedas, the śāstras, all knowledge, from Janārdana’, as will be said, verse 136); or he made the marks that set each thing apart from like and unlike; or he made the Śrīvatsa mark on his own breast.

गभस्तिनेमिः सत्त्वस्थः सिंहो भूतमहेश्वरः । आदिदेवो महादेवो देवेशो देवभृद्गुरुः ॥ ६५ ॥
gabhastinemiḥ sattvasthaḥ siṃho bhūtamaheśvaraḥ | ādidevo mahādevo deveśo devabhṛdguruḥ || 65 ||
Padaccheda
गभस्ति-नेमिः सत्त्व-स्थः सिंहः भूत-महा-ईश्वरः आदि-देवः महा-देवः देव-ईशः देव-भृत्-गुरुः
gabhasti-nemiḥ sattva-sthaḥ siṃhaḥ bhūta-mahā-īśvaraḥ ādi-devaḥ mahā-devaḥ deva-īśaḥ deva-bhṛt-guruḥ
Word by word
(He is) Gabhastinemi, (the sun) in the midst of the wheel of rays; Sattvastha, who presides over sattva (and dwells in all beings); Siṃha, (valiant as a lion); Bhūtamaheśvara, the great Lord of beings (great by the true); Ādideva, the first and god; Mahādeva, (great because he is exalted in the lordship of the yoga of Self-knowledge); Deveśa, chief Lord of the gods; Devabhṛdguru, who rules even Indra (or bears the gods and proclaims all knowledge).

Bhāṣya Gabhastinemiḥ: he stands as the sun in the middle of the wheel of rays. sattvasthaḥ: he presides chiefly over the guṇa sattva, which illumines; or he dwells in all beings. siṃhaḥ: like a lion for his valour; or the word is shortened, as Satyabhāmā is called Bhāmā. bhūtamaheśvaraḥ: the great lord of beings; or, by bhūta, the true, he is the supreme great Lord. ādidevaḥ: all beings are taken in (ādīyante) by him: he is the beginning and the god. mahādevaḥ: having given up all things, he is exalted in the great lordship of the yoga of Self-knowledge. deveśaḥ: chief lord of the gods. devabhṛdguruḥ: devabhṛt is Śakra, who bears the gods; he rules even him; or he is the guru who bears the gods and proclaims all knowledge.

उत्तरो गोपतिर्गोप्ता ज्ञानगम्यः पुरातनः । शरीरभूतभृद्भोक्ता कपीन्द्रो भूरिदक्षिणः ॥ ६६ ॥
uttaro gopatir goptā jñānagamyaḥ purātanaḥ | śarīrabhūtabhṛd bhoktā kapīndro bhūridakṣiṇaḥ || 66 ||
Padaccheda
उत्तरः गो-पतिः गोप्ता ज्ञान-गम्यः पुरातनः शरीर-भूत-भृत् भोक्ता कपि-इन्द्रः भूरि-दक्षिणः
uttaraḥ go-patiḥ goptā jñāna-gamyaḥ purātanaḥ śarīra-bhūta-bhṛt bhoktā kapi-indraḥ bhūri-dakṣiṇaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Uttara, who rises above the bondage of birth (or the highest); Gopati, (the cowherd, or the lord of the earth); Goptā, the protector of the world; Jñānagamya, reached by knowledge (not by action); Purātana, the Ancient; Śarīrabhūtabhṛt, (as prāṇa) sustaining the elements that make the body; Bhoktā, the protector (or enjoyer of supreme bliss); Kapīndra, (the Boar, or Rāma, lord of the monkeys); Bhūridakṣiṇa, whose sacrifices have abundant gifts.

Bhāṣya Uttaraḥ: he rises above (uttarati) the bondage of birth and the round; or he is highest (‘Indra is higher than all’, Ṛgveda 10.86.1). gopatiḥ: as the cowherd who tends the cows; or the lord of the earth (go). goptā: protecting all beings, the guardian of the world. jñānagamyaḥ: reached neither by action nor by knowledge and action together, but by knowledge. purātanaḥ: unbounded by time, he was even before. śarīrabhūtabhṛt: as prāṇa he sustains the elements that make up the body. bhoktā: as protector; or as enjoying the fullness of supreme bliss. Here the fifth hundred names are explained. kapīndraḥ: kapi, the Boar, and Indra: he who took the form of the Boar; or Rāghava, lord of the monkeys. bhūridakṣiṇaḥ: showing the bounds of dharma, he performs sacrifices with many gifts.

सोमपोऽमृतपः सोमः पुरुजित्पुरुसत्तमः । विनयो जयः सत्यसंधो दाशार्हः सात्वतां पतिः ॥ ६७ ॥
somapo 'mṛtapaḥ somaḥ purujit purusattamaḥ | vinayo jayaḥ satyasaṃdho dāśārhaḥ sātvatāṃ patiḥ || 67 ||
Padaccheda
सोम-पः अमृत-पः सोमः पुरु-जित् पुरु-सत्तमः विनयः जयः सत्य-संधः दाशार्हः सात्वताम् पतिः
soma-paḥ amṛta-paḥ somaḥ puru-jit puru-sattamaḥ vinayaḥ jayaḥ satya-saṃdhaḥ dāśārhaḥ sātvatām patiḥ
Word by word
(He is) Somapa, who drinks the soma (as the deity of all sacrifices); Amṛtapa, who drinks the nectar of the Self; Soma, (the moon nourishing the plants, or Śiva with Umā); Purujit, who conquers many; Purusattama, manifold (as the universe) and best; Vinaya, who chastises the wicked; Jaya, who conquers all; Satyasaṃdha, whose resolve is true; Dāśārha, worthy of gifts (or born of Daśārha's line); the Lord of the Sātvatas.

Bhāṣya Somapaḥ: he drinks the soma in all sacrifices as the deity to be worshipped; or, as the sacrificer, showing the bounds of dharma. amṛtapaḥ: he drinks the nectar of the Self; or, saving the nectar the asuras were carrying off, he made the gods drink it and drank it himself. somaḥ: as the moon he nourishes the plants; or Śiva with Umā (sa-umā). purujit: he conquers many. purusattamaḥ: puru, being the universe; sattama, being the best; one name. vinayaḥ: he chastises the wicked. jayaḥ: he conquers all beings. satyasaṃdhaḥ: whose resolve is true (‘whose resolves are true’, Chāndogya 8.1.5). dāśārhaḥ: dāśa is a gift; he deserves it; or he was born in the line of Daśārha. sātvatāṃ patiḥ: Sātvata is the name of a tantra; those who make or teach it are Sātvats; he is their lord, who bears their gain and safekeeping.

जीवो विनयितासाक्षी मुकुन्दोऽमितविक्रमः । अम्भोनिधिरनन्तात्मा महोदधिशयोऽन्तकः ॥ ६८ ॥
jīvo vinayitāsākṣī mukundo 'mitavikramaḥ | ambhonidhir anantātmā mahodadhiśayo 'ntakaḥ || 68 ||
Padaccheda
जीवः विनयितृ-साक्षी मुकुन्दः अमित-विक्रमः अम्भः-निधिः अनन्त-आत्मा महा-उदधि-शयः अन्तकः
jīvaḥ vinayitṛ-sākṣī mukundaḥ amita-vikramaḥ ambhaḥ-nidhiḥ ananta-ātmā mahā-udadhi-śayaḥ antakaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Jīva, (holding the breaths as the knower of the field); Vinayitāsākṣī, who sees directly the discipline of creatures; Mukunda, giver of liberation; Amitavikrama, whose three strides (and valour) are boundless; Ambhonidhi, (in whom the four ‘waters’, gods, men, fathers and asuras, are laid; or the ocean); Anantātmā, unbounded by place, time and thing; Mahodadhiśaya, who lies on the great ocean (into which he has gathered all beings); Antaka, who makes an end of beings.

Bhāṣya Jīvaḥ: as the knower of the field he holds the breaths. vinayitāsākṣī: he sees directly the discipline (vinayitva) of creatures; or two words: vinayitā, from nī ‘to go’, and asākṣī, he does not see anything apart from the Self as directly present. mukundaḥ: he gives liberation (mukti); the form is correct as one of the pṛṣodara group (irregular formations), and the etymologists explain it by likeness of letters. amitavikramaḥ: his three steps are unbounded, or his valour is. ambhonidhiḥ: the waters (ambhāṃsi), the gods and the others, are laid in him (‘these are the four waters: gods, men, fathers, asuras’, Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa); or the ocean (‘of lakes I am the ocean’, Gītā 10.24). anantātmā: unbounded by place, time and thing. mahodadhiśayaḥ: withdrawing all beings and making the world one ocean, he lies on it. antakaḥ: he makes an end of beings.

अजो महार्हः स्वाभाव्यो जितामित्रः प्रमोदनः । आनन्दो नन्दनोऽनन्दः सत्यधर्मा त्रिविक्रमः ॥ ६९ ॥
ajo mahārhaḥ svābhāvyo jitāmitraḥ pramodanaḥ | ānando nandano 'nandaḥ satyadharmā trivikramaḥ || 69 ||
Padaccheda
अजः महा-अर्हः स्वाभाव्यः जित-अमित्रः प्रमोदनः आनन्दः नन्दनः अनन्दः सत्य-धर्मा त्रि-विक्रमः
ajaḥ mahā-arhaḥ svābhāvyaḥ jita-amitraḥ pramodanaḥ ānandaḥ nandanaḥ anandaḥ satya-dharmā tri-vikramaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Aja, (Kāma, born from a, Viṣṇu); Mahārha, worthy of worship; Svābhāvya, ever accomplished by his nature; Jitāmitra, who has conquered the foes within and without; Pramodana, ever rejoicing (and gladdening those who meditate); Ānanda, Bliss; Nandana, who gladdens; Ananda, (who has no pleasure of objects; or Nanda, rich in all things); Satyadharmā, whose dharma (knowledge and the rest) is true; Trivikrama, whose three strides (spanned the three worlds).

Bhāṣya Ajaḥ: Kāma, born (ja) from a, Viṣṇu. mahārhaḥ: worthy of mahas, worship. svābhāvyaḥ: being ever accomplished in nature, he is not to be brought about. jitāmitraḥ: he has conquered the foes within, attachment, aversion and the rest, and without, Rāvaṇa, Kumbhakarṇa, Śiśupāla and the others. pramodanaḥ: tasting the nectar of the Self he ever rejoices; or he gladdens those who meditate on him. ānandaḥ: bliss is his nature (‘on a portion of this bliss other beings live’, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 4.3.32). nandanaḥ: he gladdens. nandaḥ: rich in all accomplishments; or anandaḥ: no worldly pleasure is his (‘the infinite is happiness; there is no happiness in the small’, Chāndogya 7.23.1). satyadharmā: his dharma, knowledge and the rest, is true. trivikramaḥ: his three strides spanned the three worlds (‘he strode three steps’, Ṛgveda 1.22.17; ‘the three worlds are called tri; you stride over them all, so you are Trivikrama’, Harivaṃśa).

Note The edition prints nandano 'nandaḥ; Śaṅkara explains both nandaḥ and anandaḥ.

महर्षिः कपिलाचार्यः कृतज्ञो मेदिनीपतिः । त्रिपदस्त्रिदशाध्यक्षो महाशृङ्गः कृतान्तकृत् ॥ ७० ॥
maharṣiḥ kapilācāryaḥ kṛtajño medinīpatiḥ | tripadas tridaśādhyakṣo mahāśṛṅgaḥ kṛtāntakṛt || 70 ||
Padaccheda
महा-ऋषिः कपिल-आचार्यः कृत-ज्ञः मेदिनी-पतिः त्रि-पदः त्रिदश-अध्यक्षः महा-शृङ्गः कृतान्त-कृत्
mahā-ṛṣiḥ kapila-ācāryaḥ kṛta-jñaḥ medinī-patiḥ tri-padaḥ tridaśa-adhyakṣaḥ mahā-śṛṅgaḥ kṛtānta-kṛt
Word by word
(He is) the great seer Kapila, the teacher (of the knowledge of the pure Self); Kṛtajña, the made (the world) and the knower (the Self); Medinīpati, Lord of the earth; Tripada, of three steps; Tridaśādhyakṣa, who oversees the three states (waking and the others); Mahāśṛṅga, (the Fish who played in the ocean of dissolution with the ship bound to his great horn); Kṛtāntakṛt, who ends what is made (and cuts off death).

Bhāṣya Maharṣiḥ kapilācāryaḥ: one name with its qualifier. He is a great seer, seeing the whole Veda, while others are seers in seeing part of it; and Kapila, the teacher of Sāṃkhya, the knowledge of the pure Self (‘the knowledge of the pure Self is called Sāṃkhya’, Vyāsa; ‘the seer Kapila, born first’, Śvetāśvatara 5.2; ‘of the perfected, the sage Kapila’, Gītā 10.26). kṛtajñaḥ: kṛta, the made, the world; jña, the knower, the Self; both. medinīpatiḥ: lord of the earth. tripadaḥ: three steps are his (‘he strode three steps’). tridaśādhyakṣaḥ: the three states, waking and the others, arising from the touching of the guṇas: he oversees them. mahāśṛṅgaḥ: as the Fish, he played in the great ocean of dissolution with the ship bound to his great horn. kṛtāntakṛt: he brings to an end what is made; or he cuts off death (kṛtānta).

महावराहो गोविन्दः सुषेणः कनकाङ्गदी । गुह्यो गभीरो गहनो गुप्तश्चक्रगदाधरः ॥ ७१ ॥
mahāvarāho govindaḥ suṣeṇaḥ kanakāṅgadī | guhyo gabhīro gahano guptaś cakragadādharaḥ || 71 ||
Padaccheda
महा-वराहः गोविन्दः सु-षेणः कनक-अङ्गदी गुह्यः गभीरः गहनः गुप्तः चक्र-गदा-धरः
mahā-varāhaḥ govindaḥ su-ṣeṇaḥ kanaka-aṅgadī guhyaḥ gabhīraḥ gahanaḥ guptaḥ cakra-gadā-dharaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Mahāvarāha, the great Boar; Govinda, (known by words, by the sentences of the Vedānta); Suṣeṇa, whose host (of attendants) is fine; Kanakāṅgadī, with golden armlets; Guhya, (to be known from the secret Upaniṣads, set in the cave of the heart); Gabhīra, deep (in knowledge, lordship and power); Gahana, hard to enter (the witness of the three states and their absence); Gupta, hidden (beyond speech and mind); Cakragadādhara, who bears the discus (the mind) and the mace (the intellect).

Bhāṣya Mahāvarāhaḥ: great and a boar. govindaḥ: he is found (vindate) by words (go), known through the sentences of the Vedānta (‘because he is to be known by words alone, he is called Govinda’, Viṣṇutilaka). suṣeṇaḥ: his host, made of his attendants, is fine. kanakāṅgadī: his armlets are of gold. guhyaḥ: as known from the secret Upaniṣads, or set in the cave, the space of the heart. gabhīraḥ: deep in knowledge, lordship, strength and power. gahanaḥ: hard to enter; or as the witness of the presence and absence of the three states. guptaḥ: being beyond the reach of speech and mind (‘this Self, hidden in all beings, does not shine forth’, Kaṭha 1.3.12). cakragadādharaḥ: ‘holding the discus, which is the principle of mind, and the mace, which is the principle of intellect, to protect the world’.

वेधाः स्वाङ्गोऽजितः कृष्णो दृढः संकर्षणोऽच्युतः । वरुणो वारुणो वृक्षः पुष्कराक्षो महामनाः ॥ ७२ ॥
vedhāḥ svāṅgo 'jitaḥ kṛṣṇo dṛḍhaḥ saṃkarṣaṇo 'cyutaḥ | varuṇo vāruṇo vṛkṣaḥ puṣkarākṣo mahāmanāḥ || 72 ||
Padaccheda
वेधाः स्व-अङ्गः अजितः कृष्णः दृढः संकर्षणः अच्युतः वरुणः वारुणः वृक्षः पुष्कर-अक्षः महा-मनाः
vedhāḥ sva-aṅgaḥ ajitaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ dṛḍhaḥ saṃkarṣaṇaḥ acyutaḥ varuṇaḥ vāruṇaḥ vṛkṣaḥ puṣkara-akṣaḥ mahā-manāḥ
Word by word
(He is) Vedhā, the Creator; Svāṅga, (himself his own instrument in action); Ajita, never conquered (in his appearances); Kṛṣṇa, (Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana); Dṛḍha, firm (never falling from his nature or power); Saṃkarṣaṇo 'cyuta, who draws in all creatures at dissolution and does not fall from his nature; Varuṇa, (the setting sun, drawing in his rays); Vāruṇa, (Vasiṣṭha or Agastya, son of Varuṇa); Vṛkṣa, standing unmoving like a tree; Puṣkarākṣa, (who pervades, and shines when meditated on in the lotus of the heart); Mahāmanā, (who creates, preserves and ends by mind alone).

Bhāṣya Vedhāḥ: the creator (vidhātṛ); the form is correct as one of the pṛṣodara group. svāṅgaḥ: in doing the work he is himself the helping limb. ajitaḥ: not conquered by anyone in his appearances. kṛṣṇaḥ: Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana (‘know Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana Vyāsa to be the lord Nārāyaṇa; who but the lotus-eyed could have made the Mahābhārata?’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa). dṛḍhaḥ: as he never falls from his nature, power and the rest. saṃkarṣaṇo 'cyutaḥ: one name with its qualifier: at dissolution he draws in all creatures at once, and does not fall from his own nature. varuṇaḥ: the setting sun, who draws in his rays (‘hear this call of mine, Varuṇa’, Ṛgveda 1.25.19). vāruṇaḥ: Vasiṣṭha or Agastya, son of Varuṇa.

Bhāṣya Vṛkṣaḥ: he stands unmoving like a tree (‘like a tree firm in heaven stands the One’, Śvetāśvatara 3.9). puṣkarākṣaḥ: from akṣ ‘to pervade’ with puṣkara before it; or, meditated on in the lotus of the heart, he shines in his own nature. mahāmanāḥ: he does creation, preservation and ending by mind alone (‘who by mind alone makes and withdraws the world’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa).

भगवान्भगहानन्दी वनमाली हलायुधः । आदित्यो ज्योतिरादित्यः सहिष्णुर्गतिसत्तमः ॥ ७३ ॥
bhagavān bhagahānandī vanamālī halāyudhaḥ | ādityo jyotirādityaḥ sahiṣṇur gatisattamaḥ || 73 ||
Padaccheda
भगवान् भग-हा आनन्दी वन-माली हल-आयुधः आदित्यः ज्योतिः-आदित्यः सहिष्णुः गति-सत्तमः
bhagavān bhaga-hā ānandī vana-mālī hala-āyudhaḥ ādityaḥ jyotiḥ-ādityaḥ sahiṣṇuḥ gati-sattamaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Bhagavān, (having the six bhagas: lordship, dharma, fame, glory, dispassion and liberation); Bhagahā, who takes away lordship and the rest at dissolution; Ānandī, blissful (full of all wealth); Vanamālī, wearing the Vaijayantī garland; Halāyudha, (as Balarāma) armed with the plough; Āditya, (born of Aditi as Vāmana); Jyotirāditya, (standing in the sun's orb); Sahiṣṇu, who bears the pairs (heat and cold); Gatisattama, the goal and the best.

Bhāṣya Bhagavān: ‘bhaga is the name of the six: entire lordship, dharma, fame, glory, dispassion and liberation’; he has them (‘he who knows the origin and end of beings, their coming and going, knowledge and ignorance, is to be called Bhagavān’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa). bhagahā: at dissolution he takes away lordship and the rest. ānandī: being of the nature of happiness; or rich in all prosperity. vanamālī: he wears the forest-garland called Vaijayantī, made of the subtle elements. halāyudhaḥ: his weapon is the plough, in the form of Balabhadra. ādityaḥ: born of Aditi and Kaśyapa as Vāmana. jyotirādityaḥ: standing in the orb of the sun, in the light. sahiṣṇuḥ: he endures the pairs, cold and heat and the rest. gatisattamaḥ: the goal and the best.

सुधन्वा खण्डपरशुर्दारुणो द्रविणप्रदः । दिवःस्पृक्सर्वदृग्व्यासो वाचस्पतिरयोनिजः ॥ ७४ ॥
sudhanvā khaṇḍaparaśur dāruṇo draviṇapradaḥ | divaḥspṛk sarvadṛgvyāso vācaspatir ayonijaḥ || 74 ||
Padaccheda
सु-धन्वा खण्ड-परशुः दारुणः द्रविण-प्रदः दिवः-स्पृक् सर्व-दृक्-व्यासः वाचः-पतिः अयोनि-जः
su-dhanvā khaṇḍa-paraśuḥ dāruṇaḥ draviṇa-pradaḥ divaḥ-spṛk sarva-dṛk-vyāsaḥ vācaḥ-patiḥ ayoni-jaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Sudhanvā, whose bow (Śārṅga) is fine; Khaṇḍaparaśu, (as Paraśurāma) whose axe cuts down foes; Dāruṇa, terrible (to the foes of the good path); Draviṇaprada, who gives devotees the wealth they wish; Divaḥspṛk, touching heaven; Sarvadṛgvyāsa, who spreads out the knowledge of all knowers (Vyāsa, who divided the Vedas); Vācaspatir Ayonija, the lord of knowledge, not born of a womb.

Bhāṣya Sudhanvā: his bow Śārṅga, made of the senses and the rest, is fine. khaṇḍaparaśuḥ: in the form of Jāmadagnya, his axe cuts (khaṇḍa) his enemies; or his axe is unbroken (akhaṇḍa). dāruṇaḥ: terrible to those who oppose the good way. draviṇapradaḥ: he gives devotees the wealth they wish. divaḥspṛk: he touches heaven. sarvadṛgvyāsaḥ: he spreads out (vyāsa) the knowledge of all knowers; or sarvadṛk, knowledge of every form, or the sight of all; and vyāsa: he divided the Veda fourfold, the Ṛgveda into twenty-one branches, the Yajurveda into a hundred and one, the Sāmaveda into a thousand and the Atharvaveda into nine, and likewise the Purāṇas; Vyāsa is Brahmā. vācaspatir ayonijaḥ: one name with its qualifier: the lord of speech, knowledge, who is not born from a mother.

त्रिसामा सामगः साम निर्वाणं भेषजं भिषक् । संन्यासकृच्छमः शान्तो निष्ठा शान्तिः परायणम् ॥ ७५ ॥
trisāmā sāmagaḥ sāma nirvāṇaṃ bheṣajaṃ bhiṣak | saṃnyāsakṛc chamaḥ śānto niṣṭhā śāntiḥ parāyaṇam || 75 ||
Padaccheda
त्रि-सामा साम-गः साम निर्वाणम् भेषजम् भिषक् संन्यास-कृत् शमः शान्तः निष्ठा शान्तिः परायणम्
tri-sāmā sāma-gaḥ sāma nirvāṇam bheṣajam bhiṣak saṃnyāsa-kṛt śamaḥ śāntaḥ niṣṭhā śāntiḥ parāyaṇam
Word by word
(He is) Trisāmā, praised by the singers with the three Sāmans called Devavrata; Sāmaga, who sings the Sāman; Sāma, the Sāmaveda; Nirvāṇa, supreme bliss where all sorrow is stilled; Bheṣaja, the remedy (for the disease of the round); Bhiṣak, the physician (who taught in the Gītā the knowledge that frees from it); Saṃnyāsakṛt, who made the fourth stage, renunciation, for liberation; Śama, who teaches calm (to renouncers); Śānta, tranquil (unattached to the pleasures of objects); Niṣṭhā, in whom beings rest at dissolution; Śānti, peace (the end of all ignorance); Parāyaṇa, the supreme abode with no fear of return.

Bhāṣya Trisāmā: praised by the Sāman-singers with the three Sāmans known as Devavrata. sāmagaḥ: he sings the Sāman (‘of the Vedas I am the Sāmaveda’, Gītā 10.22). sāma: the Sāmaveda. nirvāṇam: supreme bliss, the stilling of all sorrow. bheṣajam: the medicine for the disease of the round. bhiṣak: he taught in the Gītā the highest knowledge that frees from the disease of the round (‘I hear of you as the best of physicians’, Ṛgveda 2.33.4). saṃnyāsakṛt: for liberation he made the fourth stage of life. śamaḥ: he teaches calm, the chief means of knowledge for renouncers (‘calm is the dharma of yatis’); the form śama is from the causative ‘he does it, he teaches it’ with the suffix ac; or he calms all beings. śāntaḥ: unattached to the pleasures of objects (‘without parts, without action, tranquil’, Śvetāśvatara 6.19). niṣṭhā: at dissolution beings rest wholly in him. śāntiḥ: the ending of all ignorance. parāyaṇam: that, Brahman, is the supreme place, without fear of return; in the masculine reading the compound is a bahuvrīhi.

शुभाङ्गः शान्तिदः स्रष्टा कुमुदः कुवलेशयः । गोहितो गोपतिर्गोप्ता वृषभाक्षो वृषप्रियः ॥ ७६ ॥
śubhāṅgaḥ śāntidaḥ sraṣṭā kumudaḥ kuvaleśayaḥ | gohito gopatir goptā vṛṣabhākṣo vṛṣapriyaḥ || 76 ||
Padaccheda
शुभ-अङ्गः शान्ति-दः स्रष्टा कुमुदः कुवलेशयः गो-हितः गो-पतिः गोप्ता वृषभ-अक्षः वृष-प्रियः
śubha-aṅgaḥ śānti-daḥ sraṣṭā kumudaḥ kuvaleśayaḥ go-hitaḥ go-patiḥ goptā vṛṣabha-akṣaḥ vṛṣa-priyaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Śubhāṅga, of fair form; Śāntida, who gives peace (freedom from attachment and aversion); Sraṣṭā, the creator of all beings; Kumuda, who delights in the earth; Kuvaleśaya, who lies on the waters (or on Śeṣa); Gohita, who held up Govardhana for the good of the cows; Gopati, lord of the earth; Goptā, the protector (who also hides himself by his māyā); Vṛṣabhākṣa, whose eyes rain all desires (whose sight is dharma); Vṛṣapriya, to whom dharma is dear.

Bhāṣya Śubhāṅgaḥ: bearing a lovely body. śāntidaḥ: he gives peace, freedom from attachment, aversion and the rest. sraṣṭā: at the beginning he created all beings. kumudaḥ: he delights (mud) in the earth (ku). kuvaleśayaḥ: kuvala is water, which surrounds the earth; he lies on it; or Takṣaka lies in the jujube fruit (kuvala), and he too is Hari's glory; or kuvala is the belly of serpents, which move on the earth, and he lies on the belly of Śeṣa. gohitaḥ: for the increase of the cows he held up Govardhana; or he takes a body wishing to lighten the earth's burden. gopatiḥ: lord of the earth. goptā: protector of the world; or he hides himself by his own māyā. vṛṣabhākṣaḥ: his eyes rain all desires; or his sight is dharma. vṛṣapriyaḥ: dharma is dear to him; or he is dharma and dear.

अनिवर्ती निवृत्तात्मा संक्षेप्ता क्षेमकृच्छिवः । श्रीवत्सवक्षाः श्रीवासः श्रीपतिः श्रीमतां वरः ॥ ७७ ॥
anivartī nivṛttātmā saṃkṣeptā kṣemakṛc chivaḥ | śrīvatsavakṣāḥ śrīvāsaḥ śrīpatiḥ śrīmatāṃ varaḥ || 77 ||
Padaccheda
अनिवर्ती निवृत्त-आत्मा संक्षेप्ता क्षेम-कृत् शिवः श्रीवत्स-वक्षाः श्री-वासः श्री-पतिः श्रीमताम् वरः
anivartī nivṛtta-ātmā saṃkṣeptā kṣema-kṛt śivaḥ śrīvatsa-vakṣāḥ śrī-vāsaḥ śrī-patiḥ śrīmatām varaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Anivartī, who never turns back (from battle with the asuras, or from dharma); Nivṛttātmā, whose mind is turned from objects by nature; Saṃkṣeptā, who draws the vast world into subtle form (at dissolution); Kṣemakṛt, who guards what is gained; Śiva, who purifies by the mere remembrance of his name; Śrīvatsavakṣā, with the Śrīvatsa mark on his breast; Śrīvāsa, on whose breast Śrī dwells; Śrīpati, the lord of Śrī (and of the supreme power); the best of those who have glory (the Vedas).

Bhāṣya Anivartī: he does not turn back from the battles of gods and asuras; or, loving dharma, from dharma. nivṛttātmā: his mind is by nature turned from objects. saṃkṣeptā: at dissolution he draws the spread-out world into subtle form. kṣemakṛt: he guards what has been gained. śivaḥ: he purifies by the mere remembrance of his name. Here the sixth hundred names are explained. śrīvatsavakṣāḥ: the mark called Śrīvatsa is on his breast. śrīvāsaḥ: Śrī dwells on his breast, never leaving. śrīpatiḥ: at the churning of the nectar Śrī passed over all the gods and asuras and chose him as husband; or Śrī is the supreme power, and he is its lord (‘his supreme power is heard of as manifold’, Śvetāśvatara 6.8). śrīmatāṃ varaḥ: the chief of those whose glory is the Ṛc, Yajus and Sāman, Viriñci and the others (‘the Ṛcs, Sāmans and Yajus: that is the deathless glory of the good’).

श्रीदः श्रीशः श्रीनिवासः श्रीनिधिः श्रीविभावनः । श्रीधरः श्रीकरः श्रेयः श्रीमांल्लोकत्रयाश्रयः ॥ ७८ ॥
śrīdaḥ śrīśaḥ śrīnivāsaḥ śrīnidhiḥ śrīvibhāvanaḥ | śrīdharaḥ śrīkaraḥ śreyaḥ śrīmāṃl lokatrayāśrayaḥ || 78 ||
Padaccheda
श्री-दः श्री-ईशः श्री-निवासः श्री-निधिः श्री-विभावनः श्री-धरः श्री-करः श्रेयः श्रीमान् लोक-त्रय-आश्रयः
śrī-daḥ śrī-īśaḥ śrī-nivāsaḥ śrī-nidhiḥ śrī-vibhāvanaḥ śrī-dharaḥ śrī-karaḥ śreyaḥ śrīmān loka-traya-āśrayaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Śrīda, who gives glory to devotees; Śrīśa, Lord of Śrī; Śrīnivāsa, who ever dwells in the glorious; Śrīnidhi, in whom all glories are stored; Śrīvibhāvana, who gives beings their glories as their deeds deserve; Śrīdhara, who bears Śrī, mother of all, on his breast; Śrīkara, who makes glory for those who remember, praise and worship him; Śreya, the highest good (unfailing happiness); Śrīmān, glorious; Lokatrayāśraya, the support of the three worlds.

Bhāṣya Śrīdaḥ: he gives glory to devotees. śrīśaḥ: lord of Śrī. śrīnivāsaḥ: he ever dwells in the glorious; śrī here means those who have it. śrīnidhiḥ: all glories are stored in him, who holds all powers. śrīvibhāvanaḥ: he gives all beings various glories according to their deeds. śrīdharaḥ: he bears on his breast Śrī, the mother of all beings. śrīkaraḥ: he makes glory for devotees who remember, praise and worship him. śreyaḥ: the gaining of unfailing happiness is the highest good, and that is the very form of the supreme. śrīmān: glories are his. lokatrayāśrayaḥ: as the support of the three worlds.

स्वक्षः स्वङ्गः शतानन्दो नन्दिर्ज्योतिर्गणेश्वरः । विजितात्माविधेयात्मा सत्कीर्तिश्छिन्नसंशयः ॥ ७९ ॥
svakṣaḥ svaṅgaḥ śatānando nandir jyotirgaṇeśvaraḥ | vijitātmāvidheyātmā satkīrtiś chinnasaṃśayaḥ || 79 ||
Padaccheda
सु-अक्षः सु-अङ्गः शत-आनन्दः नन्दिः ज्योतिः-गण-ईश्वरः विजित-आत्मा अविधेय-आत्मा सत्-कीर्तिः छिन्न-संशयः
su-akṣaḥ su-aṅgaḥ śata-ānandaḥ nandiḥ jyotiḥ-gaṇa-īśvaraḥ vijita-ātmā avidheya-ātmā sat-kīrtiḥ chinna-saṃśayaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Svakṣa, whose eyes are fair as lotuses; Svaṅga, of fair limbs; Śatānanda, (the one supreme bliss divided a hundredfold by its adjuncts); Nandi, whose form is supreme bliss; Jyotirgaṇeśvara, the Lord of the hosts of lights; Vijitātmā, who has mastered his mind; Avidheyātmā, whose nature no one commands; Satkīrti, whose fame is true; Chinnasaṃśaya, in whom there is no doubt, seeing all directly.

Bhāṣya Svakṣaḥ: his eyes are fair, like lotuses. svaṅgaḥ: his limbs are fair. śatānandaḥ: the one supreme bliss is divided a hundredfold by the differences of adjuncts (‘on a portion of this bliss other beings live’, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 4.3.32). nandiḥ: his form is supreme bliss. jyotirgaṇeśvaraḥ: lord of the hosts of lights (‘him shining, all shines after’, Muṇḍaka 2.2.11; ‘the light in the sun’, Gītā 15.12). vijitātmā: who has conquered the mind. avidheyātmā: whose nature cannot be commanded by anyone. satkīrtiḥ: whose fame is true. chinnasaṃśayaḥ: having seen all directly, like an āmalaka fruit in the palm, he has no doubt anywhere.

उदीर्णः सर्वतश्चक्षुरनीशः शाश्वतस्थिरः । भूशयो भूषणो भूतिर्विशोकः शोकनाशनः ॥ ८० ॥
udīrṇaḥ sarvataścakṣur anīśaḥ śāśvatasthiraḥ | bhūśayo bhūṣaṇo bhūtir viśokaḥ śokanāśanaḥ || 80 ||
Padaccheda
उदीर्णः सर्वतः-चक्षुः अनीशः शाश्वत-स्थिरः भू-शयः भूषणः भूतिः विशोकः शोक-नाशनः
udīrṇaḥ sarvataḥ-cakṣuḥ anīśaḥ śāśvata-sthiraḥ bhū-śayaḥ bhūṣaṇaḥ bhūtiḥ viśokaḥ śoka-nāśanaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Udīrṇa, raised above all beings; Sarvataścakṣu, who sees all everywhere by his consciousness; Anīśa, who has no lord over him; Śāśvatasthira, ever being and yet never changing; Bhūśaya, (Rāma) who lay on the ground by the sea seeking the way to Laṅkā; Bhūṣaṇa, who adorns the earth with his appearances; Bhūti, being itself (the cause of all glories); Viśoka, without sorrow (being supreme bliss); Śokanāśana, who destroys the sorrow of his devotees by mere remembrance.

Bhāṣya Udīrṇaḥ: as risen above all beings. sarvataścakṣuḥ: he sees all everywhere by his own consciousness (‘with eyes everywhere’, Śvetāśvatara 3.3). anīśaḥ: none is lord over him (‘there is no lord of him’, Śvetāśvatara 6.9). śāśvatasthiraḥ: though always being, he never undergoes change; one name. bhūśayaḥ: seeking the way to Laṅkā, he lay on the ground facing the sea. bhūṣaṇaḥ: he adorns the earth with his many appearances taken by his own will. bhūtiḥ: being, existence; or glory; or as the cause of all glories. viśokaḥ: sorrow has gone from him, being one with supreme bliss. śokanāśanaḥ: by mere remembrance he destroys the sorrow of his devotees.

अर्चिष्मानर्चितः कुम्भो विशुद्धात्मा विशोधनः । अनिरुद्धोऽप्रतिरथः प्रद्युम्नोऽमितविक्रमः ॥ ८१ ॥
arciṣmān arcitaḥ kumbho viśuddhātmā viśodhanaḥ | aniruddho 'pratirathaḥ pradyumno 'mitavikramaḥ || 81 ||
Padaccheda
अर्चिष्मान् अर्चितः कुम्भः विशुद्ध-आत्मा विशोधनः अनिरुद्धः अप्रतिरथः प्रद्युम्नः अमित-विक्रमः
arciṣmān arcitaḥ kumbhaḥ viśuddha-ātmā viśodhanaḥ aniruddhaḥ apratirathaḥ pradyumnaḥ amita-vikramaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Arciṣmān, the true Radiance (by whose light the moon and sun shine); Arcita, worshipped even by Viriñci and the others; Kumbha, (in whom all rests as in a pot); Viśuddhātmā, pure, beyond the three guṇas; Viśodhana, who wipes out sins by remembrance; Aniruddha, (the fourth of the four emanations, never held back by foes); Apratiratha, who has no rival; Pradyumna, whose wealth is great; Amitavikrama, of unmeasured valour.

Bhāṣya Arciṣmān: the moon, the sun and the rest are radiant by his radiance; he alone is the true radiant one. arcitaḥ: worshipped even by Viriñci and the others whom all the worlds worship. kumbhaḥ: all rests in him as in a pot. viśuddhātmā: pure, being beyond the three guṇas, and the Self. viśodhanaḥ: by mere remembrance he wipes out sins. aniruddhaḥ: the fourth of the four emanations; or never stopped by enemies. apratirathaḥ: no opponent (pratiratha) is his. pradyumnaḥ: his wealth (dyumna) is great; or as one of the four emanations. amitavikramaḥ: his valour is unmeasured, or not hurt.

कालनेमिनिहा वीरः शौरिः शूरजनेश्वरः । त्रिलोकात्मा त्रिलोकेशः केशवः केशिहा हरिः ॥ ८२ ॥
kālaneminihā vīraḥ śauriḥ śūrajaneśvaraḥ | trilokātmā trilokeśaḥ keśavaḥ keśihā hariḥ || 82 ||
Padaccheda
कालनेमि-निहा वीरः शौरिः शूर-जन-ईश्वरः त्रि-लोक-आत्मा त्रि-लोक-ईशः केशवः केशि-हा हरिः
kālanemi-nihā vīraḥ śauriḥ śūra-jana-īśvaraḥ tri-loka-ātmā tri-loka-īśaḥ keśavaḥ keśi-hā hariḥ
Word by word
(He is) Kālaneminihā, slayer of the asura Kālanemi; Vīra, the brave; Śauri, born of Śūra's line; Śūrajaneśvara, who by his valour rules even Vāsava and the brave; Trilokātmā, the Self of the three worlds (which are not other than he); Trilokeśa, at whose command the three worlds keep to their tasks; Keśava, (whose rays, called hair, shine in the sun; or in whom Ka, Brahmā, and Īśa are); Keśihā, slayer of Keśin; Hari, who takes away the round with its cause.

Bhāṣya Kālaneminihā: he slew the asura Kālanemi. vīraḥ: valiant. śauriḥ: born in the line of Śūra. śūrajaneśvaraḥ: by his surpassing valour he rules the brave, Vāsava and the others. trilokātmā: the self of the three worlds as their inner controller; or the three worlds are not, in truth, other than he. trilokeśaḥ: at his command the three worlds keep each to its work. keśavaḥ: the rays (keśa) that enter the sun and the rest are his (‘my rays that shine are called hair; so the all-knowing call me Keśava’, Mahābhārata); or the powers named Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva are called keśa (‘three hairy ones’; in ‘my two hairs on the earth’ keśa is used for power); ‘Ka is Brahmā, and I am Īśa of all embodied beings; we were born of your parts, so you bear the name Keśava’ (Harivaṃśa). keśihā: he slew the asura Keśin. hariḥ: he takes away the round together with its cause.

कामदेवः कामपालः कामी कान्तः कृतागमः । अनिर्देश्यवपुर्विष्णुर्वीरोऽनन्तो धनंजयः ॥ ८३ ॥
kāmadevaḥ kāmapālaḥ kāmī kāntaḥ kṛtāgamaḥ | anirdeśyavapur viṣṇur vīro 'nanto dhanaṃjayaḥ || 83 ||
Padaccheda
काम-देवः काम-पालः कामी कान्तः कृत-आगमः अनिर्देश्य-वपुः विष्णुः वीरः अनन्तः धनंजयः
kāma-devaḥ kāma-pālaḥ kāmī kāntaḥ kṛta-āgamaḥ anirdeśya-vapuḥ viṣṇuḥ vīraḥ anantaḥ dhanaṃjayaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Kāmadeva, the god desired by those who seek the four ends; Kāmapāla, who guards the desires of the desirous; Kāmī, whose desires are all fulfilled; Kānta, most beautiful (or in whom Brahmā ends); Kṛtāgama, who made scripture, śruti and smṛti; Anirdeśyavapu, whose form cannot be pointed out; Viṣṇu, (whose radiance fills heaven and earth); Vīra, who moves (and the rest); Ananta, endless in place, time and thing; Dhanaṃjaya, (Arjuna, who won wealth).

Bhāṣya Kāmadevaḥ: he is desired by those who seek the four human ends, dharma and the rest, and he is god. kāmapālaḥ: he guards the desires of the desirous. kāmī: his nature is to have all desires fulfilled. kāntaḥ: bearing a most lovely body; or at the end of two parārdhas the end even of Ka, Brahmā, is from him. kṛtāgamaḥ: he made scripture, śruti, smṛti and the rest (‘śruti and smṛti are my commands’; ‘the Vedas, the śāstras, knowledge: all this from Janārdana’, as will be said). anirdeśyavapuḥ: being beyond the guṇas, his form cannot be pointed out as ‘this, such’. viṣṇuḥ: his radiance, pervading heaven and earth, is surpassing (‘my radiance stands pervading heaven and earth … by my stride I am named Viṣṇu’, Mahābhārata).

Bhāṣya Vīraḥ: having going and the rest (the root vī: going, begetting, shining, casting, eating). anantaḥ: being all-pervading, eternal and the Self of all, unbounded by place, time and thing (‘Brahman is truth, knowledge, infinite’, Taittirīya 2.1); or ‘gandharvas, apsarases, siddhas … do not reach the end of his qualities; so he is Ananta’ (Viṣṇu Purāṇa). dhanaṃjayaḥ: Arjuna, who won much wealth in his conquest of the quarters (‘of the Pāṇḍavas I am Dhanaṃjaya’, Gītā 10.37).

ब्रह्मण्यो ब्रह्मकृद्ब्रह्मा ब्रह्म ब्रह्मविवर्धनः । ब्रह्मविद्ब्राह्मणो ब्रह्मी ब्रह्मज्ञो ब्राह्मणप्रियः ॥ ८४ ॥
brahmaṇyo brahmakṛd brahmā brahma brahmavivardhanaḥ | brahmavid brāhmaṇo brahmī brahmajño brāhmaṇapriyaḥ || 84 ||
Padaccheda
ब्रह्मण्यः ब्रह्म-कृत् ब्रह्मा ब्रह्म ब्रह्म-विवर्धनः ब्रह्म-वित् ब्राह्मणः ब्रह्मी ब्रह्म-ज्ञः ब्राह्मण-प्रियः
brahmaṇyaḥ brahma-kṛt brahmā brahma brahma-vivardhanaḥ brahma-vit brāhmaṇaḥ brahmī brahma-jñaḥ brāhmaṇa-priyaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Brahmaṇya, good to brahman (austerity, the Vedas, brāhmaṇas, knowledge); Brahmakṛt, the maker of austerity and the rest; Brahmā, who creates all as Brahmā; Brahman, (great, truth, knowledge, infinite); Brahmavivardhana, who increases austerity and the rest; Brahmavit, who knows the Veda and its meaning; Brāhmaṇa, (who as the brāhmaṇa teaches the Veda to the worlds); Brahmī, to whom all called brahman belongs; Brahmajña, who knows the Vedas as his own Self; Brāhmaṇapriya, dear to brāhmaṇas, to whom they are dear.

Bhāṣya Brahmaṇyaḥ: ‘austerity, the Vedas, brāhmaṇas and knowledge are called brahman’; he is good to them. brahmakṛt: maker of austerity and the rest. brahmā: as Brahmā he creates all. brahma: great and making great, of the nature of truth and the rest (‘Brahman is truth, knowledge, infinite’; ‘that knowledge, in which all difference has set, pure being, beyond words, known to itself, is called Brahman’, Viṣṇu Purāṇa). brahmavivardhanaḥ: he increases austerity and the rest. brahmavit: he knows the Veda and its meaning as they are. brāhmaṇaḥ: as a brāhmaṇa he teaches all the worlds, saying ‘this is (the teaching) of the Veda’ (vedasyāyam iti; the 1910 print has the misprint vedakhyāyam). brahmī: all that is called brahman belongs to him. brahmajñaḥ: he knows the Vedas as his own Self. brāhmaṇapriyaḥ: dear to brāhmaṇas, or brāhmaṇas are dear to him (‘he who does not bow duly to a brāhmaṇa even when struck, cursed, or spoken to harshly is a sinner, burnt by the fire of brahman, … not mine’, the Lord; Mahābhārata).

महाक्रमो महाकर्मा महातेजा महोरगः । महाक्रतुर्महायज्वा महायज्ञो महाहविः ॥ ८५ ॥
mahākramo mahākarmā mahātejā mahoragaḥ | mahākratur mahāyajvā mahāyajño mahāhaviḥ || 85 ||
Padaccheda
महा-क्रमः महा-कर्मा महा-तेजाः महा-उरगः महा-क्रतुः महा-यज्वा महा-यज्ञः महा-हविः
mahā-kramaḥ mahā-karmā mahā-tejāḥ mahā-uragaḥ mahā-kratuḥ mahā-yajvā mahā-yajñaḥ mahā-haviḥ
Word by word
(He is) Mahākrama, of great strides; Mahākarmā, whose works (creation and the rest) are great; Mahātejā, whose light makes the sun and others shine; Mahoraga, the great serpent (Vāsuki); Mahākratu, the great sacrifice (the horse sacrifice); Mahāyajvā, the great sacrificer (for the good of the world); Mahāyajña, the great worship (the japa-sacrifice); Mahāhavi, the great oblation (the world offered in the Self).

Bhāṣya Mahākramaḥ: his strides are great (‘may Viṣṇu, the wide-striding, give us welfare’). mahākarmā: his work, the creation of the world and the rest, is great. mahātejāḥ: by his light the sun and the other shining ones shine (‘by whose light the sun burns, kindled’; Gītā 15.12); or he is adorned with great qualities, valour and the rest. mahoragaḥ: a great serpent (‘of serpents I am Vāsuki’, Gītā 10.28). mahākratuḥ: a great sacrifice (‘as the horse sacrifice, king of sacrifices’, Manu 11.260); that too is he: praise. mahāyajvā: for the holding together of the world he performs sacrifices. mahāyajñaḥ: the great sacrifice (‘of sacrifices I am the japa-sacrifice’, Gītā 10.25). mahāhaviḥ: the great oblation: the whole world, offered in the Self, which is Brahman, as the Self. Or these, mahākratu and the rest, are bahuvrīhis.

स्तव्यः स्तवप्रियः स्तोत्रं स्तुतिः स्तोता रणप्रियः । पूर्णः पूरयिता पुण्यः पुण्यकीर्तिरनामयः ॥ ८६ ॥
stavyaḥ stavapriyaḥ stotraṃ stutiḥ stotā raṇapriyaḥ | pūrṇaḥ pūrayitā puṇyaḥ puṇyakīrtir anāmayaḥ || 86 ||
Padaccheda
स्तव्यः स्तव-प्रियः स्तोत्रम् स्तुतिः स्तोता रण-प्रियः पूर्णः पूरयिता पुण्यः पुण्य-कीर्तिः अनामयः
stavyaḥ stava-priyaḥ stotram stutiḥ stotā raṇa-priyaḥ pūrṇaḥ pūrayitā puṇyaḥ puṇya-kīrtiḥ anāmayaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Stavya, praised by all and praising none; Stavapriya, fond of praise; Stotra, the hymn (that tells his qualities); Stuti, the act of praising; Stotā, the praiser too; Raṇapriya, to whom battle is dear (holding the five weapons to protect the world); Pūrṇa, full (of all desires and powers); Pūrayitā, who fills all with riches; Puṇya, who wipes out sins by remembrance; Puṇyakīrti, whose fame brings merit; Anāmaya, untouched by ills within and without.

Bhāṣya Stavyaḥ: praised by all, praising none. stavapriyaḥ: hence fond of praise. stotram: that by which he is praised, the telling of his qualities: that too is Hari. stutiḥ: the act of praising. stotā: the praiser too is he. raṇapriyaḥ: battle is dear to him, since he always bears the five great weapons to protect the world. pūrṇaḥ: full of all desires and all powers. pūrayitā: not only full, but filling all with riches. puṇyaḥ: by mere remembrance he wipes out sins. puṇyakīrtiḥ: his fame brings merit to men. anāmayaḥ: not afflicted by ills, inner or outer, that are born of action.

मनोजवस्तीर्थकरो वसुरेता वसुप्रदः । वसुप्रदो वासुदेवो वसुर्वसुमना हविः ॥ ८७ ॥
manojavas tīrthakaro vasuretā vasupradaḥ | vasuprado vāsudevo vasur vasumanā haviḥ || 87 ||
Padaccheda
मनः-जवः तीर्थ-करः वसु-रेताः वसु-प्रदः वसु-प्रदः वासुदेवः वसुः वसु-मनाः हविः
manaḥ-javaḥ tīrtha-karaḥ vasu-retāḥ vasu-pradaḥ vasu-pradaḥ vāsudevaḥ vasuḥ vasu-manāḥ haviḥ
Word by word
(He is) Manojava, swift as the mind (being everywhere); Tīrthakara, the maker and teacher of the fourteen branches of knowledge (and of the doctrines outside them); Vasuretā, whose seed is gold; Vasuprada, who gives wealth directly; Vasuprada, who gives his devotees the highest wealth, liberation; Vāsudeva, the son of Vasudeva; Vasu, (in whom beings dwell, and who dwells in them); Vasumanā, whose mind dwells equally in all things; Havi, the oblation (Brahman).

Bhāṣya Manojavaḥ: being everywhere, his speed is like the mind's. tīrthakaraḥ: the maker and teacher of the fourteen branches of knowledge and of the systems outside them; the Purāṇa-tellers relate that as Hayagrīva, having slain Madhu and Kaiṭabha, he taught Viriñci at the beginning of creation the Vedas and the other branches of knowledge, and taught the systems outside the Veda to delude the enemies of the gods. vasuretāḥ: gold (vasu) is his seed (‘the god first created the waters and cast his seed in them; it became a golden egg, the supreme cause of Brahmā’, Vyāsa). vasupradaḥ: he gives wealth abundantly, being himself the lord of wealth; the other (Kubera) is lord of wealth by his grace.

Bhāṣya Vasupradaḥ (the second): he gives his devotees the highest wealth, the fruit called liberation (‘Brahman is knowledge, bliss, the final goal of the giver’, Bṛhadāraṇyaka 3.9.28); or he utterly destroys (pra-do) the wealth of the gods' enemies. vāsudevaḥ: the son of Vasudeva. vasuḥ: beings dwell in him, and he dwells in them. vasumanāḥ: his mind dwells (vas) equally in all objects. haviḥ: (‘the offering is Brahman, the oblation is Brahman’, Gītā 4.24).

सद्गतिः सत्कृतिः सत्ता सद्भूतिः सत्परायणः । शूरसेनो यदुश्रेष्ठः सन्निवासः सुयामुनः ॥ ८८ ॥
sadgatiḥ satkṛtiḥ sattā sadbhūtiḥ satparāyaṇaḥ | śūraseno yaduśreṣṭhaḥ sannivāsaḥ suyāmunaḥ || 88 ||
Padaccheda
सत्-गतिः सत्-कृतिः सत्ता सत्-भूतिः सत्-परायणः शूर-सेनः यदु-श्रेष्ठः सत्-निवासः सु-यामुनः
sat-gatiḥ sat-kṛtiḥ sattā sat-bhūtiḥ sat-parāyaṇaḥ śūra-senaḥ yadu-śreṣṭhaḥ sat-nivāsaḥ su-yāmunaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Sadgati, reached by the good (those who know ‘Brahman is’), or whose understanding is good; Satkṛti, whose work (guarding the world) is good; Sattā, being (pure experience without difference within or without); Sadbhūti, (the supreme Self, real because never sublated, and shining); Satparāyaṇa, the supreme goal of those who know the truth; Śūrasena, whose army (Hanūmān and the others) is valiant; Yaduśreṣṭha, the best of the Yadus; Sannivāsa, the abode of the wise; Suyāmuna, (attended by the fair folk of the Yamunā).

Bhāṣya Sadgatiḥ: ‘if one knows that Brahman is, then they know him as being’ (Taittirīya 2.6); those who know Brahman as being are the good (santaḥ), and he is reached by them; or his understanding is good. satkṛtiḥ: his work, protecting the world, is good. Here the seventh hundred names are explained. sattā: experience free of difference within, from things of its kind and of other kinds (‘one only, without a second’, Chāndogya 6.2.1). sadbhūtiḥ: the supreme Self, consciousness, is being, since it is not sublated and it shines; nothing else is, since it is known and is sublated: it is neither being nor non-being; the sublation of the manifold meant is that by scripture or by reasoning.

Bhāṣya Satparāyaṇaḥ: the highest abode of the good, those who know the truth. śūrasenaḥ: his army, in which Hanūmān and other soldiers are valiant. yaduśreṣṭhaḥ: as the chief of the Yadus. sannivāsaḥ: the refuge of the wise. suyāmunaḥ: those of the Yamunā, Devakī, Vasudeva, Nanda, Yaśodā, Balabhadra, Subhadrā and others, who surround him, are fair; or the cowherd folk of the Yamunā, Padmāsana and others, are his fair attendants.

भूतावासो वासुदेवः सर्वासुनिलयोऽनलः । दर्पहा दर्पदो दृप्तो दुर्धरोऽथापराजितः ॥ ८९ ॥
bhūtāvāso vāsudevaḥ sarvāsunilayo 'nalaḥ | darpahā darpado dṛpto durdharo 'thāparājitaḥ || 89 ||
Padaccheda
भूत-आवासः वासुदेवः सर्व-असु-निलयः अनलः दर्प-हा दर्प-दः दृप्तः दुर्धरः अथ अपराजितः
bhūta-āvāsaḥ vāsudevaḥ sarva-asu-nilayaḥ analaḥ darpa-hā darpa-daḥ dṛptaḥ durdharaḥ atha aparājitaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Bhūtāvāsa, in whom beings dwell; Vāsudeva, (who covers the world by māyā, and is god); Sarvāsunilaya, in whom all the breaths come to rest; Anala, whose powers have no ‘enough’; Darpahā, who destroys the pride of those on the wrong path; Darpada, who gives pride (honour) to those on the path of dharma; Dṛpta, ever joyful (tasting the nectar of the Self); Durdhara, hardly held in the heart; and Aparājita, never conquered.

Bhāṣya Bhūtāvāsaḥ: beings dwell in him (‘beings dwell in you, so you are Bhūtāvāsa’, Harivaṃśa). vāsudevaḥ: he covers (vas) the world by māyā, and is god (‘I cover the whole world, becoming as the sun with its rays’, the Lord). sarvāsunilayaḥ: all the breaths (asu) are dissolved in him, their ground, the self of the jīva. analaḥ: there is no ‘enough’ to his wealth of powers. darpahā: he destroys the pride of those who stand on paths against dharma. darpadaḥ: he gives pride to those who walk the way of dharma. dṛptaḥ: tasting the nectar of the Self, he is ever joyful.

Bhāṣya Durdharaḥ: being free of all adjuncts he cannot be held in meditation; yet by his grace some hold him in the heart with difficulty, by contemplation over thousands of births (‘the trouble is greater for those whose minds are set on the unmanifest’, Gītā 12.5). aparājitaḥ: not defeated by inner foes, attachment and the rest, nor by outer ones, the dānavas.

विश्वमूर्तिर्महामूर्तिर्दीप्तमूर्तिरमूर्तिमान् । अनेकमूर्तिरव्यक्तः शतमूर्तिः शताननः ॥ ९० ॥
viśvamūrtir mahāmūrtir dīptamūrtir amūrtimān | anekamūrtir avyaktaḥ śatamūrtiḥ śatānanaḥ || 90 ||
Padaccheda
विश्व-मूर्तिः महा-मूर्तिः दीप्त-मूर्तिः अमूर्तिमान् अनेक-मूर्तिः अव्यक्तः शत-मूर्तिः शत-आननः
viśva-mūrtiḥ mahā-mūrtiḥ dīpta-mūrtiḥ amūrtimān aneka-mūrtiḥ avyaktaḥ śata-mūrtiḥ śata-ānanaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Viśvamūrti, whose form is the universe (being the Self of all); Mahāmūrti, of great form (lying on Śeṣa); Dīptamūrti, whose form is knowledge, shining; Amūrtimān, who has no body bound by action; Anekamūrti, who takes many forms (to help the worlds); Avyakta, not manifest (as just this); Śatamūrti, of a hundred forms (born of the many notions of consciousness); Śatānana, hundred-faced.

Bhāṣya Viśvamūrtiḥ: the universe is his form, as he is the Self of all. mahāmūrtiḥ: his form, lying on the couch of Śeṣa, is great. dīptamūrtiḥ: his form is made of knowledge, shining; or the bright form he takes by his own will is shining. amūrtimān: no body bound by action is his. anekamūrtiḥ: in his appearances he takes by his will many forms that help the worlds. avyaktaḥ: though he has many forms, it is not made manifest that he is just such. śatamūrtiḥ: the forms born of various notions belong to him whose form is consciousness. śatānanaḥ: since he has the form of the universe and the rest, he is hundred-faced.

एको नैकः सवः कः किं यत्तत्पदमनुत्तमम् । लोकबन्धुर्लोकनाथो माधवो भक्तवत्सलः ॥ ९१ ॥
eko naikaḥ savaḥ kaḥ kiṃ yat tat padam anuttamam | lokabandhur lokanātho mādhavo bhaktavatsalaḥ || 91 ||
Padaccheda
एकः नैकः सवः कः किम् यत् तत् पदम् अनुत्तमम् लोक-बन्धुः लोक-नाथः माधवः भक्त-वत्सलः
ekaḥ naikaḥ savaḥ kaḥ kim yat tat padam anuttamam loka-bandhuḥ loka-nāthaḥ mādhavaḥ bhakta-vatsalaḥ
Word by word
(He is) Eka, the One (free of all difference); Naika, not one (many by māyā); Sava, the sacrifice in which soma is pressed; Ka, happiness; Kim, what is to be inquired into; Yat, (that from which beings arise); Tat, that (which extends); the unsurpassed State; Lokabandhu, the kinsman of the worlds; Lokanātha, the lord of the worlds; Mādhava, born in the line of Madhu; Bhaktavatsala, loving to his devotees.

Bhāṣya Ekaḥ: in truth free of all difference, of its kind, of other kinds and within (‘one only, without a second’, Chāndogya 6.2.1). naikaḥ: by māyā he has many forms (‘Indra by his māyās goes in many forms’). savaḥ: the sacrifice in which soma is pressed. kaḥ: ka means happiness; he is praised by it (‘Brahman is ka’, Chāndogya 4.10.5). kim: Brahman, to be inquired into, being all the human ends. yat: the word that points to a thing established of itself names Brahman (‘that from which these beings are born’, Taittirīya 3.1). tat: Brahman, which extends (tan) (‘Om tat sat: this is the threefold designation of Brahman’, Gītā 17.23). padam anuttamam: one name with its qualifier: the state reached by seekers of liberation, than which there is nothing higher.

Bhāṣya Lokabandhuḥ: all the worlds are bound (badh) in him, their ground; or, as the father of the worlds, there is no kinsman like him; or he gave the teaching of what is good and bad, śruti and smṛti. lokanāthaḥ: the worlds pray to him; or he afflicts (upatapati), blesses or rules them. mādhavaḥ: born in the line of Madhu. bhaktavatsalaḥ: full of love for his devotees.

सुवर्णवर्णो हेमाङ्गो वराङ्गश्चन्दनाङ्गदी । वीरहा विषमः शून्यो घृताशीरचलश्चलः ॥ ९२ ॥
suvarṇavarṇo hemāṅgo varāṅgaś candanāṅgadī | vīrahā viṣamaḥ śūnyo ghṛtāśīr acalaś calaḥ || 92 ||
Padaccheda
